3.1
–
3.2
, and the Hölder inequality, we have

(3.46)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta rJ_{9}|_{2}&\leq C\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}\big|_{4}\big|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|_{4}+C\big|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big|_{\infty}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\end{aligned}

(3.47)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta rJ_{10}|_{2}&\leq C(|\zeta rU_{tt}|_{2}+|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}|\zeta rU_{t}|_{2})+C|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{4}|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{4}\\
&\quad+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big(\big|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big|_{\infty}|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{2}+|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|_{2}\big)\leq C(|\zeta rU_{tt}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})),\end{aligned}

(3.48)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\zeta rJ_{11}|_{2}\leq C\big(|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}\big|_{\infty}+|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{4}\big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).\end{aligned}

Hence, collecting (
3.44
) and (
3.46
)–(
3.48
), together with Lemmas
3.4
and
A.11
, gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.49)

\int_{0}^{t}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|\zeta rU_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

1.2.
It follows from (
3.35
) by applying
D_{\bar{\eta}}
that

(3.50)

\zeta D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{2U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)=\zeta J_{12},

where
J_{12}
has controls of the following form:

\displaystyle|\zeta J_{12}|

\displaystyle\leq C\zeta\Big((|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|+|\bar{\Lambda}||D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|+|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}||U_{t}|+|\bar{\Lambda}|^{2}|U_{t}|)

\displaystyle+|\bar{\Lambda}|\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U|+|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})|\big)+\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}|^{3}\big)\big|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U\big|

\displaystyle+(|D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}|^{2})\big(|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U|+\big|D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big|\big)

\displaystyle+|D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}|^{3}+|\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|+|\bar{\Lambda}||\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}|+|D_{\bar{\eta}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}|\Big).

Then, due to the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\sim 1-r
, (
3.1
), (
3.18
), Lemmas
3.2
,
3.5
, and
A.5
, and the Hölder inequality, we obtain that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.51)

\displaystyle|\zeta rJ_{12}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|_{2}+|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{4}|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{t}|_{4}+|\bar{\Lambda}|^{2}_{\infty}|\zeta rU_{t}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big(|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{2}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{3}\big)(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}+1)

\displaystyle+C\big(\big|\zeta_{\frac{5}{8}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{4}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}^{2}\big)|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U|_{4}+\big|(\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi},D_{\bar{\eta}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi})\big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\Big|\zeta r\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}})\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})),

where the following inequality has also been used:

|\zeta r^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,U_{t})|_{4}\leq\big|\zeta r^{\frac{5}{4}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}),U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Hence, collecting (
3.50
)–(
3.51
) gives that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.52)

\Big|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{2U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big|_{2}\leq C(|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})).

Next, we multiply (
3.16
) by
\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}
and apply
D_{\bar{\eta}}
to the resulting equality to obtain

\displaystyle\zeta D_{\bar{\eta}}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{2U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\zeta J_{13},

where
J_{13}
has controls of the following form:

\displaystyle|\zeta J_{13}|

\displaystyle\leq C\zeta\Big(\big|D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\big|+|\bar{\Lambda}|\big|\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}}\big|+\big(\big|D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\big|+|\bar{\Lambda}|\big|\frac{\bar{\Lambda}}{\bar{\eta}}\big|\big)\big(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|+1\big)

\displaystyle+\big|\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big||\bar{\Lambda}|+\Big|\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi},\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}\big)\Big|\Big).

Then, following the calculations (
3.50
)–(
3.52
), we can similarly obtain

(3.53)

\Big|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\eta}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{2U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\zeta rD_{\bar{\eta}}(\frac{U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}})\big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big).

Finally, combining (
3.52
)–(
3.53
), along with Lemma
A.11
and (
3.49
), implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.54)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Ct\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Step 2.
L^{1}(0,T)
-boundedness of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U)
.

2.1.
First, applying
\partial_{t}
to (
2.58
) yields

\displaystyle\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\sharp}J_{14},

where
J_{14}
has controls of the following form:

\displaystyle|\chi^{\sharp}J_{14}|

\displaystyle\leq|D_{\bar{\eta}}U||D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|+|U||\bar{U}|+|U_{t}|+|\rho_{0}^{\gamma-\delta}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|+\frac{|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|}{\rho_{0}^{\delta}}\Big|\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\hat{r}^{2}\rho_{0}}{\bar{\eta}^{2}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\Big|

\displaystyle+\frac{|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|}{\rho_{0}^{\delta}}\Big|\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}^{\delta}|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\Big|+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}^{\delta}}\Big|\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}^{\delta}(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U||\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|+|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|)\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\Big|

\displaystyle+\frac{|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|}{\rho_{0}^{\delta}}\Big|\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\Big|++\frac{1}{\rho_{0}^{\delta}}\Big|\int_{r}^{1}\rho_{0}(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}||U_{t}|,U_{tt})\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\Big|.

Then, using an argument similar to (
2.57
)–(
2.59
), together with the fact that
(3-2\varepsilon_{0})(\gamma-1)-\delta>0
, Lemmas
3.1
,
3.5
, and
A.5
, and the Hölder inequality, we can obtain that,

\displaystyle\,\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}+|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}U_{t}|_{2}+|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})-\delta}|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}|_{2}\big|_{2}|\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})-\delta}|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}|_{2}\big|_{2}\big(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}+|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}U_{t},U_{tt})|_{2}\big)

(3.55)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}.

As a consequence, recalling (
3.44
)–(
3.45
), we obtain from (
1.23
), (
3.1
), (
3.41
)–(
3.42
), (
3.55
), and Lemmas
3.1
–
3.2
and
A.5
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{9}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{U}\big|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U)\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C|(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{10}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U_{tt},|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U}|_{\infty}U_{t})\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C|\chi^{\sharp}\bar{\Lambda}_{t}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U)\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big(|(\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U,U)\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},U_{t})\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{11}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}\big|_{2}|(1,\bar{U},D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{U})|_{\infty}|\chi^{\sharp}(\bar{\Lambda},\bar{\Lambda}_{t},\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi})|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\end{aligned}

which, along with (
3.44
) and Lemmas
3.3
–
3.4
, leads to

(3.56)

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}+C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\big).

Finally, integrating the above over
[0,t]
, together with Lemma
3.4
, implies that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.57)

\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

2.2.

First, recall the derivation of (
2.72
):

(3.58)

\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}:=(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)_{r}+\big(\frac{\delta}{\gamma-1}+2\big)\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1})_{r}}{\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U=\sum_{i=15}^{18}J_{i},

where

\displaystyle J_{15}

\displaystyle:=-\big(1+\frac{2\delta}{\gamma-1}\big)\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U-\frac{\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}U+\frac{2a_{2}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+(\delta+2(\gamma-1))\bar{\eta}_{r}\frac{D_{\bar{\eta}}\mathscr{J}}{\mathscr{J}}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U,

\displaystyle J_{16}

\displaystyle:=-2\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}\big(1+\frac{\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{2a_{2}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\big)D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-2\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}\Big(\big(2+\frac{\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{a_{2}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\big)\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)+\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}\big(\frac{U}{\bar{\eta}}\big)\Big),

\displaystyle J_{17}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma+\delta-2}}\Big(\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\,+\frac{1-\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}\big)U_{t}+2\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t}+\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\Big),

\displaystyle J_{18}

\displaystyle:=\frac{A\gamma}{(2a_{1}+a_{2})(\gamma-1)}\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{1-\delta-2(\gamma-1)}\Big(\bar{\varrho}^{2(\gamma-1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}+2\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}+\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{1-\delta}{\gamma-1}\bar{\Lambda}^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{-2(\gamma-1)+1-\delta}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}\big(D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\,+\frac{1-\delta}{\gamma-1}\frac{\bar{\Lambda}^{2}}{\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}}\big)\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}

(3.59)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\bar{\eta}_{r}\bar{\varrho}^{-2(\gamma-1)+1-\delta}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\Big(2\frac{\gamma-\delta}{\gamma-1}\bar{\varrho}^{\gamma-1}\bar{\Lambda}D_{\bar{\eta}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}+\bar{\varrho}^{2(\gamma-1)}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi}\Big).

For the right-hand side of the above, it follows from the facts that

\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\sim 1-r,\quad\big(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)(\gamma-1)+1-\delta>0,\quad\big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\big)(\gamma-1)-2(\gamma-1)+1-\delta>-\frac{\gamma-1}{2},

(
1.23
), (
3.1
), (
3.42
), and Lemmas
3.1
–
3.5
and
A.5
that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{15}\big|_{2}&\leq C|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C|\chi^{\sharp}\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{16}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}|\chi^{\sharp}(U,D_{\eta}U)|_{\infty}\\
&\quad+C(1+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U,D_{\bar{\eta}}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U,D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U)\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\end{aligned}

(3.60)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{17}\big|_{2}&\leq C(|\chi^{\sharp}D_{\bar{\eta}}\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty}+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t})\big|_{2}\\
&\quad+C(1+|\bar{\Lambda}|_{\infty})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(D_{\bar{\eta}}U_{t},D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t})\big|_{2}\\
&\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}J_{18}\big|_{2}&\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\bar{\Lambda}\big|_{2}+|(\bar{\Lambda}^{2},\bar{\Lambda})|_{\infty}\big)\times\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\big|\big(1,\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi},\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi},\chi_{a}^{\sharp}(D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}\overline{D_{\eta}\Phi})^{2}\big)\big|_{\infty}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).\end{aligned}

Therefore, substituting (
3.60
) into (
3.58
) gives

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big),

which, along with Lemmas
3.5
and
D.1
, yields

\displaystyle\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|(\zeta-\chi)\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathrm{cross}}\big|_{2}+C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{3}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(1+\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big).

Finally, it follows from the
L^{1}(0,T)
-estimate of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)
and (
3.57
) that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(t+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

Step 3.
L^{\infty}(0,T)
-boundedness for
t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)
.
Following a similar argument in Steps 1–2 gives

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}(|\zeta rU_{tt}|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})),\qquad\sqrt{t}\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\big(\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\big(\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}+\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})\big),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{4}U\big|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{t}\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\bar{\eta}}^{2}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\big).

Therefore, this, combined with Lemma
3.4
, leads to the desired result of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

For any
0\leq t\leq T_{3}=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\}
,

(3.61)

\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Proof.

Collecting the estimates established in Lemmas
3.3
–
3.6
, we see that, for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(3.62)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}).

First, (
2.3
), together with (
3.2
) and Lemma
3.5
, yields that, for any
0\leq t\leq T_{3}:=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\}
,

(3.63)

|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{r}U|_{\infty}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{\bar{\eta}}U|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})T_{3}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Next, let
(\mathring{\mathcal{E}},\mathring{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
be defined in the same way as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})(t,f)
in (
1.24
) and (
1.27
), respectively, except for letting
\eta(r)=r
. Then, based on (
3.62
)–(
3.63
), we obtain from Lemma
C.1
that,
for all
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(\bar{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\bar{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0})\Big(\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

which thus leads to
\eqref{39''-0}_{1}
.
∎

Hence, define constants
(c_{1},T^{*})
as

(3.64)

c_{1}:=C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}),\qquad T^{*}:=T_{3}=\min\{T_{2},(2C\mathfrak{p}(c_{0}))^{-1}\},

then we obtain from Lemma
3.7
that, for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

(3.65)

\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\qquad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

4.
Local Well-Posedness of the Nonlinear Problem

In this section, we prove the local well-posedness of classical solutions of (
1.22
) stated in Theorem
1.1
.
For convenience, in the rest of §
4
, we let
(\mathring{\mathcal{E}},\mathring{\mathcal{D}})(t,f)
and
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{k}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}})(t,f)
be defined in the same way as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})(t,f)
in (
1.24
) and (
1.27
), except with
\eta(r)
in place of
r
and
\eta^{\mathrm{k}}
, respectively, where
\eta^{\mathrm{k}}
denotes the
\mathrm{k}
-th generation of the iterative sequence that will be given later (see also Appendix
C
).

4.1.
Introduction of the Picard Iteration

Let
c_{0}
be given as in (
3.1
) and

U^{0}(t,r):=u_{0},\qquad\eta^{0}(t,r)=r+tu_{0}.

Then there exists a small positive time
T^{\prime}\leq T^{*}
, with
T^{*}
defined in (
3.64
),
such that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(4.1)

\mathcal{E}_{0}(t,U^{0})+t\mathcal{D}_{0}(t,U^{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{0}(s,U^{0})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{0}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{0}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Next, set
\bar{\eta}=\eta^{0}
in (
2.1
). By Lemma
2.1
and (
2.3
), problem (
2.1
) admits a unique classical solution
(U^{1},\eta^{1})
in
[0,T^{\prime}]\times\bar{I}
. Certainly, it follows from (
3.65
) that
(v^{1},\eta^{1})
also satisfies the following uniform estimates on
[0,T^{\prime}]
:

(4.2)

\mathcal{E}_{1}(t,U^{1})+t\mathcal{D}_{1}(t,U^{1})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{1}(s,U^{1})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{1}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{1}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

As a consequence, the approximate sequence
(U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1})
(
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
) can be constructed iteratively as follows: Given
(U^{\mathrm{k}},\eta^{\mathrm{k}})
, define
(U^{\mathrm{k}+1},\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1})
by solving the following problem in
(0,T^{\prime}]\times I
:

(4.3)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{2}\rho_{0}U_{t}^{\mathrm{k+1}}+A\big((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma}\big)_{r}-2A\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma}+4\pi G\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\hat{r}^{2}\rho_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r}\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle\ =\Big((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\delta}\big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)\Big)_{r}\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad\quad-\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\delta}\Big(2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}_{r}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+4(a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big),\\[8.0pt]
\eta_{t}^{\mathrm{k+1}}=U^{\mathrm{k+1}},\\[8.0pt]
(U^{\mathrm{k+1}},\eta^{\mathrm{k+1}})|_{t=0}(r)=\big(u_{0}(r),r\big)\qquad\text{for }r\in I,\end{cases}

where
\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}
is given by

\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}=\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}.

By (
3.65
), we obtain an iterative solution sequence
(U^{\mathrm{k}},\eta^{\mathrm{k}})
satisfying (
4.1
), that is, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4.4)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{k}}(s,U^{\mathrm{k}})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2},\\[4.0pt]
&U^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{r=0}=\big(D_{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}U^{\mathrm{k}}+\frac{2a_{2}}{2a_{1}+a_{2}}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)\Big|_{r=1}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Of course, by Lemma
C.1
, (
4.4
) also implies

(4.5)

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U^{\mathrm{k}})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U^{\mathrm{k}})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\quad|\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

4.2.
Strong Convergence of the Approximation Solutions.

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}

\displaystyle:=U^{\mathrm{k}+1}-U^{\mathrm{k}},\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1}:=\eta^{\mathrm{k}+1}-\eta^{\mathrm{k}}=\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma-1},\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k+1}}\!:=\psi^{\mathrm{k+1}}-\psi^{\mathrm{k}},

and introduce the following energy function:

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Then, based on (
4.3
), the problem of
(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1})
can be written as

(4.6)

\!\!\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}-\Big((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\delta}\big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+2a_{2}\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)\Big)_{r}\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\delta}\Big(2a_{2}\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k+1}}_{r}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+4(a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big)=\big((r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{h^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}\big)_{r}+(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{g^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}},\\[8.0pt]
\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1}_{t}=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1},\widehat{\eta}^{\mathrm{k}+1})(r)\big|_{t=0}=(0,0)\qquad\text{for }r\in I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle h^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\big((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}\big)^{(1-\delta)}\Big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{-(1+\delta)}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}})^{-(1+\delta)}}\big)

\displaystyle+2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}})^{-\delta}}\big)\Big)-A\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}\big(\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k}}+\psi^{\mathrm{k-1}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r}})\big)\Big),

\displaystyle g^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle:=-(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\big((\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}\big)^{(1-\delta)}\Big(2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{-\delta}}\big)

\displaystyle+4(a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}\big)\Big)-2A\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}\big(\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k}}+\psi^{\mathrm{k-1}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-4\pi G\frac{r\rho_{0}^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2}}\big)\int_{0}^{r}\hat{r}^{2}\rho_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r}.

Since

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\ \big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\big)_{t}=\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}-1}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\big)\\
&\implies\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}=1+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Big(-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}-1}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\,\mathrm{d}s,\end{aligned}

it follows from (
4.4
) that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(4.7)

\Big|\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)^{2}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{r}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Similarly, we can also obtain

(4.8)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}-1\Big|+\Big|\big(\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)^{2}-1\Big|+\Big|\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{-(1+\delta)}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}})^{-(1+\delta)}}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}|\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}})^{-\delta}}-1\Big|+\Big|\frac{(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathrm{k}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Moreover, since

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k}}_{t}

\displaystyle=-(\gamma-1)\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k}}\big(\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{2U^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)-(\gamma-1)\psi^{\mathrm{k-1}}\Big(\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-2(\gamma-1)\psi^{\mathrm{k-1}}\Big(\frac{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}+\frac{U^{\mathrm{k}-1}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k-1}}}\big)\Big),

it follows from (
4.4
) and (
4.7
)–(
4.8
) that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(4.9)

|\widehat{\psi}^{\mathrm{k}}|\leq Ce^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, it follows from (
4.4
), (
4.7
)–(
4.9
), and the Hölder and Minkowski inequalities that, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and
\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

(4.10)

\displaystyle|(g^{\mathrm{k}},h^{\mathrm{k}})(t)|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Now, multiplying
\eqref{398}_{1}
by
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, then using an argument similar to
L_{1}
in (
2.21
), we obtain from (
4.4
), (
4.10
), and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}+1}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k+1}}\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C|(g^{\mathrm{k}},h^{\mathrm{k}})|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}\big|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Integrating the above over
[0,t]
leads to

\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}+1}(t)\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t^{2}e^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(t)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T^{\prime}]$ and $\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

Choosing
t=T_{*}
in the above inequality such that

C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})T_{*}^{2}e^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})T_{*}}\leq\frac{1}{2},\qquad T_{*}\leq T^{\prime},

leads to

(4.11)

\sum_{\mathrm{k}=1}^{\infty}\widehat{E}_{\mathrm{k}}(T_{*})\leq\Big(\sum_{\mathrm{k}=0}^{\infty}2^{-\mathrm{k}}\Big)\widehat{E}_{1}(T_{*})\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Then (
4.11
) implies that
\{U^{\mathrm{k}}\}_{\mathrm{k}\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence that converges to some limit
U
as
\mathrm{k}\to\infty
in the following sense:

\displaystyle(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U^{\mathrm{k}}\to(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}U

\displaystyle\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

\displaystyle(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}U^{\mathrm{k}}\to(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}U

\displaystyle\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

which also leads to

(4.12)

U^{\mathrm{k}}\to U\qquad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}})\qquad\text{as $\mathrm{k}\to\infty$}.

On the other hand, from (
4.5
) and
\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\sim 1-r
, it follows that, for any
t\in[0,T_{*}]
and
a\in(0,1)
,

(4.13)

t\|U^{\mathrm{k}}\|_{H^{4}(a,1-a)}^{2}\leq C(a)(\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U))\leq C(a)\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}).

Hence, it follows (
4.12
)–(
4.13
) and Lemma
A.3
that, for any
a\in(0,1)
,

(4.14)

U^{\mathrm{k}}\to U\qquad\text{in }L^{1}([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}})\ \ \text{as $\mathrm{k}\to\infty$}.

Now, based on (
4.14
) and
\eqref{396}_{2}
, we have

(4.15)

\eta^{\mathrm{k}}=r+\int_{0}^{t}U^{\mathrm{k}}\,\mathrm{d}s\to r+\int_{0}^{t}U\,\mathrm{d}s\qquad\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}).

This also implies that
\{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}})
which converges to some limit
\eta
so that

\eta=r+\int_{0}^{t}U\,\mathrm{d}s\qquad\text{for {a.e.} $(t,r)\in(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)$}.

To recover equation
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
, we first divide
\eqref{396}_{1}
by
(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}
to derive

\displaystyle U_{t}^{\mathrm{k+1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varrho^{\mathrm{k}}\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big((\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\delta}\big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U_{r}^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}+2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\big)-A(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{\gamma}\Big)_{r}-4\pi G\frac{1}{(\eta^{\mathrm{k}})^{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\hat{r}^{2}\rho_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\frac{4a_{1}}{(\varrho^{\mathrm{k}})^{1-\delta}\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big(\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}_{r}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathrm{k}}}-\frac{U^{\mathrm{k+1}}}{\eta^{\mathrm{k}}}\Big).

Then, due to Lemma
A.4
,
\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\sim 1-r
,
\rho^{\gamma-1}_{0}\in H^{3}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, and (
4.14
)–(
4.15
), we take the limit as
k\to\infty
in the above and obtain

\displaystyle U_{t}^{\mathrm{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varrho}D_{\eta}\Big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\varrho^{\delta}\big(D_{\eta}U+\frac{2U}{\eta}\big)-A\varrho^{\gamma}\Big)-D_{\eta}\Phi-4a_{1}\frac{D_{\eta}(\varrho^{\delta})}{\varrho}\frac{U}{\eta},

which, along with the uniqueness of limits, implies that
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
holds for
a.e.

(t,r)\in(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)
. Moreover, by the lower semi-continuity of weak convergence and (
4.5
), we have

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,U)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1}),\quad|\eta_{r}(t)-1|_{\infty}+\Big|\frac{\eta(t)}{r}-1\Big|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

This completes the proof of the existence.

4.3.
Uniqueness and Time Continuity

Let
(U^{\mathsf{a}},\eta^{\mathsf{a}})
and
(U^{\mathsf{b}},\eta^{\mathsf{b}})
be two solutions of (
1.22
) in
(0,T_{*})\times(0,1)
. Define

(\widehat{\eta},\widehat{U}):=(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}-\eta^{\mathsf{a}},U^{\mathsf{b}}-U^{\mathsf{a}}),\quad\,\,\widehat{E}(t):=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

It follows from (
1.22
) that
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})
solves the following problem in
(0,T_{*}]\times I
:

(4.16)

\!\!\begin{cases}\displaystyle r^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{U}_{t}-\Big((\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}(\varrho^{\mathsf{b}})^{\delta}\big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathsf{b}}}+2a_{2}\frac{\widehat{U}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\big)\Big)_{r}\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{\mathsf{b}}\eta_{r}^{\mathsf{b}}(\varrho^{\mathsf{b}})^{\delta}\Big(2a_{2}\frac{\widehat{U}_{r}}{\eta_{r}^{\mathsf{b}}}+4(a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{\widehat{U}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\Big)=\big((r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tilde{h}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big)_{r}+(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\tilde{g}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}},\\[8.0pt]
\widehat{\eta}_{t}=\widehat{U},\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})(r)\big|_{t=0}=(0,0)\qquad\text{for }r\in I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{h}

\displaystyle:=(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\big((\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}\big)^{(1-\delta)}\Big((2a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U_{r}^{\mathsf{a}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{-(1+\delta)}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2(1-\delta)}(\eta_{r}^{\mathsf{b}})^{-(1+\delta)}}\big)

\displaystyle+2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathsf{a}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta_{r}^{\mathsf{b}})^{-\delta}}\big)\Big)-A\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}\big(\widehat{\psi}+\psi^{\mathsf{a}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}})\big)\bigg),

\displaystyle\tilde{g}

\displaystyle:=-(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\big((\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}\big)^{(1-\delta)}\Big(2a_{2}\frac{U^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2(1-\delta)-1}(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r})^{-\delta}}\big)

\displaystyle+4(a_{1}+a_{2})\frac{U^{\mathsf{a}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{-2\delta}(\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r})^{1-\delta}}\big)\Big)-2A\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}\big(\widehat{\psi}+\psi^{\mathsf{a}}(1-\frac{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{a}}})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\pi G\frac{r\rho_{0}^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}}{\eta^{\mathsf{b}}}\big(1-\frac{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{2}}\big)\int_{0}^{r}\hat{r}^{2}\rho_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{r},

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathsf{a}}:=\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{a}})^{2}\eta^{\mathsf{a}}_{r}}\big)^{\gamma-1},\qquad\psi^{\mathsf{b}}:=\big(\frac{r^{2}\rho_{0}}{(\eta^{\mathsf{b}})^{2}\eta^{\mathsf{b}}_{r}}\big)^{\gamma-1},\qquad\widehat{\psi}:=\psi^{\mathsf{b}}-\psi^{\mathsf{a}}.

Similarly, we can show that
(\tilde{h},\tilde{g})
satisfy (
4.10
) with
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{k}}
replaced by
\widehat{U}
. Hence, by the same arguments as in Step 2.2, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})te^{C\mathfrak{p}(c_{1})t}\int_{0}^{t}\big|(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{r}\widehat{U}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, together with the Grönwall inequality, yields that
\widehat{E}(t)\equiv 0
,
i.e.
,
U^{\mathsf{a}}\equiv U^{\mathsf{b}}
.

Now, we show that
(U,\eta)
are classical satisfying
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
. First, the regularity of
U
and the boundary condition shown in (
1.32
) can be proved by the same argument as in Steps 7–8 in §
2.3
. Then the regularity of
\eta
follows directly from the formula:
\eta_{t}=U
. Finally, following a similar argument in Step 9 in §
2.3
, we can show that
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{1}
holds pointwise in
(0,T_{*}]\times\bar{I}
.

Appendix A
Some Basic Lemmas

For the convenience of readers, we list some basic facts that have been used frequently in this paper. Throughout the following Appendices
A
–
D
, let
\rho_{0}(\bm{y})=\rho_{0}(r)
, with
\rho_{0}(r)
satisfy (
1.20
), that is, for some constants
K_{2}>K_{1}>0
and for all
r\in I
,

r\big(\rho_{0},\mathscr{D}_{r}^{j}((\rho^{\gamma-1}_{0})_{r})\big)\in L^{2}(I)\quad\text{for $j=0,1,2$},\qquad K_{1}(1-r)\leq\rho^{\gamma-1}_{0}(r)\leq K_{2}(1-r).

The first two lemmas concern the separability and density of the weighted Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.1

(
[
20
]
)
.

Let
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}\in(-1,\infty)
, and let
\tilde{d}=\tilde{d}(r)=r^{\vartheta_{1}}(1-r)^{\vartheta_{2}}
be a function defined on
I
. Then, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
H^{k}_{\tilde{d}}
is a reflexive separable Banach space. Moreover, if
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
is dense in
H^{k}_{\tilde{d}}
with respect to the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{k,\tilde{d}}
.

Lemma A.2
.

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}
with
\upsilon\geq 0
is a reflexive separable Banach space.
Moreover, for any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}
, there exists
a sequence
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}}
such that

\|f^{\varepsilon}-f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from Lemma
A.1
. For the convergence, define
\bm{f}(\bm{y}):=f(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
. Clearly, thanks to Lemma
B.1
in Appendix
B
and the fact that
\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\sim 1-r
, we have
\bm{f}\in H^{1}(B_{a})
for any
a\in(0,1)
.

Now, we claim that there exists a sequence
\{\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}(\bm{y})=f^{\varepsilon}_{\flat}(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}(B_{\frac{3}{4}})
, such that

(A.1)

\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}\to\bm{f}\qquad\text{in $H^{1}(B_{\frac{3}{4}})$ as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Indeed, let
\{\omega_{\varepsilon}(\bm{y})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
be the spherically symmetric mollifier defined on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then
\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}(\bm{y}):=(\bm{f}*\omega_{\varepsilon})(\bm{y})
(with
\varepsilon
sufficiently small) satisfies (
A.1
). Thus, by Lemma
A.8
, in order to verify that
\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}
is spherically symmetric, we only need to show that
\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})=(\mathcal{O}\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon})(\bm{y})
for any matrix
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
. In fact, we have

\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})=\int_{\Omega}\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y}-\bm{z})\omega_{\varepsilon}(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}\qquad\text{for $0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{100}$ and $\bm{y}\in B_{\frac{3}{4}}$}.

Changing the coordinate
\bm{z}\mapsto\mathcal{O}\bm{z}
, along with the facts that
|\mathcal{O}\bm{y}|=|\bm{y}|
and
\det\mathcal{O}=1
, gives

\displaystyle\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y}-\mathcal{O}\bm{z})\omega_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}\bm{z})(\det\mathcal{O})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\int_{\Omega}\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}(\bm{y}-\bm{z}))\omega_{\varepsilon}(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}(\mathcal{O}\bm{f})(\bm{y}-\bm{z})\omega_{\varepsilon}(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}=\Big(\mathcal{O}\big(\int_{\Omega}\bm{f}(\cdot-\bm{z})\omega_{\varepsilon}(\bm{z})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{z}\big)\Big)(\bm{y})=(\mathcal{O}\bm{f}_{\flat}^{\varepsilon})(\bm{y}).

This completes the proof of the claim.

Consequently, it follows from (
A.1
) and Lemma
B.1
that

(A.2)

f^{\varepsilon}_{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}}(B_{\frac{3}{4}}),\qquad\,\,\|\zeta f^{\varepsilon}_{\flat}-\zeta f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$},

where the cut-off function
\zeta
is defined in §1.2.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
A.1
that there exists a smooth sequence
\{f_{\sharp}^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}[\frac{1}{3},1]
such that

(A.3)

\|\zeta^{\sharp}f^{\varepsilon}_{\sharp}-\zeta^{\sharp}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}\leq C\|\zeta^{\sharp}f^{\varepsilon}_{\sharp}-\zeta^{\sharp}f\|_{1,\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Therefore, defining
f^{\varepsilon}:=\zeta f_{\flat}^{\varepsilon}+\zeta^{\sharp}f_{\sharp}^{\varepsilon}
, we see that
f^{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}}
. Then we can obtain from (
A.2
)–(
A.3
) that

\|f^{\varepsilon}-f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}\leq\|\zeta f^{\varepsilon}_{\flat}-\zeta f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}+\|\zeta^{\sharp}f^{\varepsilon}_{\sharp}-\zeta^{\sharp}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{\upsilon(\gamma-1)}}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

This completes the proof.
∎

The third lemma concerns the well-known interpolation theory for the
H^{k}
spaces.

Lemma A.3

(
[
21
]
)
.

Let
J\subset\mathbb{R}
be some open interval and
F\in H^{p}(J)\cap H^{q}(J)

(p,q\geq 0)
. Then
F\in H^{l}(J)
for
l=p\vartheta+q(1-\vartheta)
and
0\leq\vartheta\leq 1
, and the following inequality holds
:

\left\|F\right\|_{l}\leq C\left\|F\right\|_{p}^{\vartheta}\left\|F\right\|_{q}^{1-\vartheta},

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
(p,q,\vartheta)
.

The fourth lemma is on the classical Sobolev embedding theorems.

Lemma A.4

(
[
21
]
)
.

We state the Sobolev embeddings in the one-dimensional and three-dimensional cases separately.

(i)

Let
J\subset\mathbb{R}
be some open interval and
f=f(r)
be some function on
J
. Then there exist two positive constants
(s_{0},C)
, depending only on the Lebesgue measure of
J
, such that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{1}(J)}+C\|f_{r}\|_{L^{1}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in W^{1,1}(J),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}(J)}+C\|f_{r}\|_{L^{2}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in H^{1}(J),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(J)}\leq s_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}(J)}+C\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(J)}\|f_{r}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(J)}

\displaystyle\text{for all }f\in H^{1}(J).

In particular,
W^{1,1}(J)\hookrightarrow C(\bar{J})
and
H^{1}(J)\hookrightarrow C(\bar{J})
continuously
;
moreover,
if
f(r_{0})=0
for some
r_{0}\in\bar{J}
, then
s_{0}=0
can be chosen in the above.

(ii)

Let
J\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be some bounded domain with smooth boundary. Assume that
f\in H^{1}(J)
, then there exists a positive constant
C(J)
depending only on
J
, such that

\|f\|_{L^{6}(J)}\leq C(J)\,\|f\|_{H^{1}(J)}.

In particular, if
J=\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then for any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, there exists a positive universal constant
C_{0}
such that

\|f\|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C_{0}\,\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

The next two lemmas are on the Hardy inequality and some weighted interpolation inequality. In Lemmas
A.5
–
A.6
, we let
0\leq a<b<\infty
and
J=(a,b)
, and let
d=d(r)
be some function on
J
, taking one of the following two forms:

d(r)=r-a,\qquad\text{or}\ \ d(r)=b-r.

Lemma A.5

(
[
2
,
33
]
)
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{p}

(\vartheta>0\text{ if }p=\infty)
. Then, for any
f
such that
d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{2}(J)
,

(i)

If
p\in[1,2)
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
, depending only on
(\varepsilon,p,a,b,\vartheta)
, such that

\|d^{\vartheta+\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C_{1}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{2}(J)};

(ii)

If
p\in[2,\infty]
, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
, which depends only on
(p,a,b,\vartheta)
if
p\neq\infty
and depends only on
(a,b,\vartheta)
if
p=\infty
, such that

\|d^{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C_{2}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}.

Lemma A.6

(
[
4
]
)
.

Let
p\in[2,\infty]
and
\vartheta>-\frac{1}{p}

(\vartheta>0\text{ if }p=\infty)
. Then, for any
f
satisfying
d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{2}(J)
, there exists a constant
C>0
, which depends only on
(p,a,b,\vartheta)
if
p\neq\infty
and depends only on
(a,b,\vartheta)
if
p=\infty
, such that

(A.4)

\|d^{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{p}(J)}\leq C\big(\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}+\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|d^{\vartheta+\frac{1}{p}}f_{r}\big\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

The seventh lemma is used to obtain the time-weighted estimates of the velocity.

Lemma A.7

(
[
1
]
)
.

Let
f\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
. Then there exists a sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{k}\rightarrow 0,\quad\,\,t_{k}|f(t_{k})|^{2}_{2}\rightarrow 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\rightarrow\infty$}.

The eighth lemma is an equivalent statement for the spherical symmetry of vector functions.

Lemma A.8

(
[
4
]
)
.

Let
\bm{f}=\bm{f}(\bm{y})
be a spherically symmetric continuous vector function on
B_{R}=\{\bm{y}:\,|\bm{y}|<R\}
for some
R>0
. Then
\bm{f}
takes the form
:

\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(|\bm{y}|)\frac{\bm{y}}{|\bm{y}|}
if and only if

(A.5)

\mathcal{O}\bm{f}(\bm{y})=\bm{f}(\mathcal{O}\bm{y})\qquad\text{for all $\bm{y}\in B_{R}$ and $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

In particular, any spherically symmetric vector function
\bm{f}
satisfies
\bm{f}(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
.

For giving the time continuity of the velocity in our proof, the following two types of evolution triple embedding are required.

Lemma A.9

(
[
11
]
)
.

Let
T>0
and
J\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}

(k=1,2,3)
be some open subset. Assume that
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{0}(J))
and
f_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}(J))
.
Then
f\in C([0,T];L^{2}(J))
, and the map
:

t\mapsto\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{2}
is absolutely continuous
with

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(J)}^{2}=2\left<f_{t},f\right>_{H^{-1}(J)\times H_{0}^{1}(J)}\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Moreover, if additionally
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H_{0}^{1}(J))
, then
f\in C([0,T];L^{4}(J))
.

Lemma A.10

(
[
4
]
)
.

Let
T>0
,
s\geq 0
, and

\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}},\qquad\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow L^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{*},

where
\mathcal{H}
is a Banach space. Assume that
f\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H})
and
r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{*})
. Then
f\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}})
, and the map
:

t\mapsto|f(t)|_{2,r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}^{2}
is absolutely continuous with

(A.6)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|f(t)|_{2,r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}^{2}=2\left<r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t},f\right>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}.

Proof.

This lemma can be obtained by basically following the proof of Theorem 3 on page 303
in
[
11
]
*Chapter 5, and we only sketch it here. After extending
f(t,\cdot)
by zero from
[0,T]
to
\mathbb{R}
, we can regularize
f
with respect to
t
via the convolution:

f^{\varepsilon}(t,r):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f(t-\tau,r)\omega_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where
\omega_{\varepsilon}
is the standard mollifier. Thus, for any
\varepsilon,\sigma>0
, we have

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=2\big<r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma},f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}.

Integrating the above over
[0,T]
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\varepsilon}(0)-f^{\sigma}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}+\big\|r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Since
L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}
,
\mathcal{H}
, and
\mathcal{H}^{*}
are all Banach spaces due
to Lemma
A.1
, by Theorem 8.20 in
[
21
]
*Chapter 8, for all
g_{1}(0)\in L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}
,
g_{2}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H})
, and
g_{3}\in L^{2}([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{*})
, we have

\begin{gathered}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\|g_{1}^{\varepsilon}(0)-g_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|g_{2}^{\varepsilon}-g_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\|g_{3}^{\varepsilon}-g_{3}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t=0.\end{gathered}

Hence, letting
(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)
, together with the fact that
(r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f)*\omega_{\varepsilon}=r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f^{\varepsilon}
, yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)-f^{\sigma}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\|f^{\varepsilon}(0)-f^{\sigma}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\lim_{(\varepsilon,\sigma)\to(0,0)}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|f^{\varepsilon}-f^{\sigma}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}+\big\|r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}-r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\sigma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{*}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t=0,

which shows that
f^{\varepsilon}
converges to
f
in
C([0,T];L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}})
.
Similarly, we have

\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=\|f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\big<r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t}^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $\tau,t\in[0,T]$}.

Taking the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
implies

(A.7)

\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}=\|f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\big<r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}f_{t},f\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{*}\times\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime},

which implies the absolute continuity of
\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{r^{2}\rho_{0}^{s}}}^{2}
. Applying
\partial_{t}
to (
A.7
) yields (
A.6
).
∎

Finally, we give a variant of the div-curl estimates for spherically symmetric functions.

Lemma A.11
.

Let
\eta
be the classical solution obtained in
Theorem
1.1
, and let
f=f(r)
be a smooth function defined on
I
. Then the following norms are equivalent
:
for any
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}f|_{2,r^{2}}\sim\Big|\zeta_{a}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2,r^{2}},

\displaystyle|\zeta_{a}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{k}f|_{2,r^{2}}\sim\Big|\zeta_{a}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{k-2}\Big(D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2,r^{2}}\quad\text{for $k=2,3,4$},

where
F_{1}\sim F_{2}
denotes that there exists a constant
C\geq 1

(
independent of
(a,\eta,f)
)
such that
C^{-1}F_{1}\leq F_{2}\leq CF_{1}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we only give the proof for
a=\frac{1}{2}
and show that

\big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{4}f\big|_{2}\leq C\Big|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}\Big(D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}.

Besides, the following fact will be used frequently later:

D_{\eta}\zeta\leq 0\qquad\text{for all $(t,r)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

First, a direct calculation gives

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}^{3}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\big(|D_{\eta}^{4}f|^{2}+4\big|D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r+\underline{4\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}D_{\eta}^{4}fD_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:={AL}_{1}}.

Then, for
{AL}_{1}
, note that the following identity holds:

D_{\eta}^{4}f=\eta D_{\eta}^{4}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+4D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big).

Hence, this, together with integration by parts, yields

\displaystyle{AL}_{1}

\displaystyle=16\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\big|D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+4\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{3}\eta_{r}D_{\eta}^{4}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=10\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\big|D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r-4\int_{0}^{1}\zeta D_{\eta}\zeta\eta^{3}\eta_{r}\big|D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\geq 0,

which, along with (
A.8
), gives

(A.9)

\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}^{3}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\geq\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}|D_{\eta}^{2}(\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}f)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Next, notice that

(A.10)

D_{\eta}\big(\frac{D_{\eta}^{2}f}{\eta}\big)=D_{\eta}^{3}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+2D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)=\eta D_{\eta}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)+4D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big).

Hence, this, together with a direct calculations, implies

\displaystyle\,\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

(A.11)

\displaystyle=\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\eta D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big)+6D_{\eta}\big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\Big(\Big|\eta D_{\eta}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}+36\Big|D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\underline{12\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{3}\eta_{r}D_{\eta}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}r}_{:={AL}_{2}},

where
AL_{2}
can be handled by integration by parts:

\displaystyle AL_{2}

\displaystyle=-18\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\Big|D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r-12\int_{0}^{1}\zeta D_{\eta}\zeta\eta^{3}\eta_{r}\Big|D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\geq-18\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\Big|D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Therefore, (
A.11
), combined with the above, implies

(A.12)

\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}\geq 18\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\Big|D_{\eta}\Big(\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Finally, it follows from the above and (
A.9
)–(
A.10
) that

(A.13)

\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{2}\eta^{2}\eta_{r}\Big|D_{\eta}\big(\frac{D_{\eta}^{2}f}{\eta}\big)\Big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C\Big|\zeta(\eta^{2}\eta_{r})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}\Big(D_{\eta}\big(D_{\eta}f+\frac{2f}{\eta}\big)\Big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Hence, by (
A.9
), (
A.12
)–(
A.13
), and the lower and upper bounds of
(\frac{\eta}{r},\eta_{r})
, we obtain the desired estimates.
∎

Appendix B
Coordinate Transformations

Generally, it is desirable to consider the Lagrangian formulation, so that we can pullback (
1.1
) on the moving domain
\Omega(t)
to a problem on a fixed domain
\Omega
. To this end, denote by
\bm{x}=\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y})
the position of the fluid particle
\bm{x}\in\Omega(t)
at time
t\geq 0
so that

(B.1)

\bm{\eta}_{t}(t,\bm{y})=\bm{u}(t,\bm{\eta}(t,\bm{y}))\ \ \text{for $t>0$},\qquad\text{with $\bm{\eta}(0,\bm{y})=\bm{y}$},

and
(t,\bm{y})
are the 3-D Lagrangian coordinates.
This appendix is devoted to showing the conversion of some Sobolev spaces between the 3-D Lagrangian coordinates
\bm{y}
and the corresponding spherical coordinate
r=|\bm{y}|
for spherically symmetric functions.

Let
0\leq a<b
,
\mathcal{J}:=\{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\,a\leq|\bm{y}|<b\}
, and
r=|\bm{y}|\in J:=[a,b)
. Consider a coordinate transformation
\bm{\xi}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})\in C^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{J}})
such that

\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\xi(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}\quad\text{with $\xi(r)\geq 0$},\qquad\bm{\xi}:\mathcal{J}\to\mathcal{G}:=\bm{\xi}(\mathcal{J}),\qquad\bm{y}\mapsto\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}).

Assume that
\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi}
is a non-singular matrix, and define

\begin{gathered}D_{\xi}f=\frac{f_{r}}{\xi_{r}},\qquad\mathcal{B}=(\mathcal{B}_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\qquad\text{with $\mathcal{B}_{ij}:=((\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{-1})_{ij}$},\\[-2.0pt]
\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f=((\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{1},(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{2},(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{3})^{\top}\qquad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}f)_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{B}_{ki}\partial_{y_{k}}f$},\\[-3.0pt]
\bm{f}=(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3})^{\top},\qquad\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f}=((\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f})_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\qquad\text{with $(\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}\bm{f})_{ij}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{B}_{kj}\partial_{y_{k}}f_{i}$},\end{gathered}

where
f(\bm{y})=f(r)
and
\bm{f}(\bm{y})=f(r)\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
are sufficiently smooth functions.

Then we have the following coordinate transformations.

Lemma B.1
.

Assume that
(g,\bm{g})(\bm{x})
are spherically symmetric functions defined on
\mathcal{G}
and
(f,\bm{f})(\bm{y})
satisfy

f(\bm{y})=g(\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}))=g(\bm{x}),\qquad\bm{f}(\bm{y})=\bm{g}(\bm{\xi}(\bm{y}))=\bm{g}(\bm{x}).

Then, for any
q\in[1,\infty]
, the following statements hold
:

(i)

Transformations for
(g,f)
:
for
j=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\|(\xi^{2}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}f\|_{L^{q}(J)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}g\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{j}f\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\|(\xi^{2}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathscr{D}_{\xi}^{j-1}(D_{\xi}f)\|_{L^{q}(J)};

(ii)

Transformations for
(\bm{g},\bm{f})
:
for
j=0,1,2,3,4
,

\|\nabla^{j}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}\sim\|(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})^{\frac{1}{q}}\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{j}\bm{f}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{J})}\sim\|(\xi^{2}\xi_{r})^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathscr{D}_{\xi}^{j}f\|_{L^{q}(J)}.

Here,
E\sim F
denotes
C^{-1}E\leq F\leq CE
for some universal constant
C\geq 1
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the transformations for
(\bm{g},\bm{f})
, since
\nabla_{\bm{y}}f=f_{r}\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
can be regarded as a vector function
\bm{h}=h\frac{\bm{y}}{r}
with
h=f_{r}
. Moreover, for simplicity, we only sketch the proof for the highest-order estimates. We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
We first prove the case when
\xi(r)=r
. In this case,
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\bm{y}
and
\mathcal{B}
is the
n\times n
identity matrix. It follows from direct calculations that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (f_{k})_{y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}}&=\frac{y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}f_{rrrr}\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{\delta_{ip}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jp}y_{i}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{kp}y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}y_{p}}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{3}}-\frac{10y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}\Big)\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr}\\
&\quad+\Big(\frac{\delta_{i\ell}\delta_{jp}y_{k}+\delta_{i\ell}\delta_{kp}y_{j}+\delta_{j\ell}\delta_{ip}y_{k}+\delta_{j\ell}\delta_{kp}y_{i}+\delta_{k\ell}\delta_{ip}y_{j}+\delta_{k\ell}\delta_{jp}y_{i}}{r}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}\delta_{kp}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ij}\delta_{\ell p}y_{k}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jp}y_{\ell}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{\ell p}y_{j}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{ip}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}}{r}\\
\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\quad\ \ \begin{aligned} &\quad\quad\ \ +\frac{\delta_{ij}\delta_{k\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{j\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{i\ell}y_{p}}{r}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{ip}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{jp}y_{i}y_{k}y_{\ell}+\delta_{kp}y_{i}y_{j}y_{\ell}+\delta_{\ell p}y_{i}y_{j}y_{k})}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{i\ell}y_{j}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{j\ell}y_{i}y_{k}y_{p}+\delta_{k\ell}y_{i}y_{j}y_{p})}{r^{3}}\\
&\quad\quad\ \ -\frac{3(\delta_{ij}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{ik}y_{j}y_{\ell}y_{p}+\delta_{jk}y_{i}y_{\ell}y_{p})}{r^{3}}+\frac{15y_{i}y_{j}y_{k}y_{\ell}y_{p}}{r^{5}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r},\end{aligned}

where
\delta_{ij}
denotes the Kronecker symbol with indices
(i,j)
:
\delta_{ij}=1
if
i=j
,
\delta_{ij}=0
if
i\neq j
. Then the above expressions yield

|\nabla_{\bm{y}}^{4}\bm{f}|^{2}=|f_{rrrr}|^{2}+20\Big|\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rrr}\Big|^{2}+120\Big|\Big(\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}\Big)_{r}\Big|^{2}.

Moreover, since
(\frac{f_{rr}}{r})_{r}=(\frac{f}{r})_{rrr}+2(\frac{1}{r}(\frac{f}{r})_{r})_{r}
, we obtain from the above that

(B.2)

|\nabla^{4}_{\bm{y}}\bm{f}|\sim|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{4}f|.

Finally, denote
\omega_{3}
as the surface area of the
3
-sphere,
due to the integral identity:

\int_{\mathcal{J}}f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{y}=\omega_{3}\int_{J}f(r)r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r,

we thus obtain the desired conclusions of this lemma when
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})=\bm{y}
from (
B.2
).

2.
For general
\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
, we can first repeat the calculations in Step 1 with the coordinate
\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
and the function
\bm{g}(\bm{x})
. Specifically, if we let
x:=|\bm{x}|
, then
x=\xi(r)\in W
and
W:=[\xi(a),\xi(b))
, and we can obtain from (
B.2
) that

\|\nabla^{4}\bm{g}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{G})}\sim\big\|x^{\frac{2}{q}}\mathscr{D}_{x}^{4}g\big\|_{L^{q}(W)}.

Next, using the coordinate transformations
\bm{x}=\bm{\xi}(\bm{y})
and
x=\xi(r)
, we have
\nabla^{k}\bm{g}=\nabla_{\mathcal{B}}^{k}\bm{f}
and
\partial_{x}^{k}g=D_{\xi}^{k}f
for
k=0,1,2,3,4
. Therefore, the following integral identities

\int_{\mathcal{G}}g(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{x}=\int_{\mathcal{J}}f(\bm{y})(\det\nabla_{\bm{y}}\bm{\xi})\,\mathrm{d}\bm{y},\qquad\int_{W}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{J}f(r)\xi_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r,

lead to the desired results of this lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Remarks on the Energy Functionals

This appendix is devoted to giving some equivalent forms of the energy functionals. In what follows, we always let
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
be the initial data of
IBVP
(
1.22
),
\rho_{0}
satisfy (
1.20
), and
u_{0}
satisfy (
1.29
).

Define

(C.1)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t,f)

\displaystyle=\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\zeta r(f,\mathscr{D}_{r}f,f_{t},\mathscr{D}_{r}f_{t})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta r(\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}f,\mathscr{D}_{r}^{3}f)(t)\big|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(f,f_{t})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(f_{r},f_{tr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(f_{rr},f_{rrr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2},

and

(C.2)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}(t,U)

\displaystyle=\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,U)+\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,U),

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\zeta r(f_{tt},\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}f_{t},\mathscr{D}_{r}^{4}f)(t)\big|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)

\displaystyle:=\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(f_{trr},f_{rrrr})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}.

Clearly, we have

\mathring{\mathcal{X}}(0,f)=\mathcal{X}(0,f)\qquad\text{for $\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}},\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}$}.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
T>0
, and let
\eta
be defined by
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{2}
and
(\eta,U)
satisfy

(C.3)

\begin{gathered}(\eta_{r},\frac{\eta}{r})\in[\delta_{*},\delta^{*}],\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{E}(t,U)=\mathrm{E}_{1}<\infty,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\mathcal{E}(t,U)+t\mathcal{D}(t,U))+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}(s,U)\,\mathrm{d}s=\mathrm{E}_{2}<\infty\end{gathered}

for some given positive constants
(\delta_{*},\delta^{*},\mathrm{E}_{1},\mathrm{E}_{2})
. Assume that
f=f(t,r)
is a function defined on
[0,T]\times I
such that

(C.4)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\mathcal{E}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}(t,f))+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty.

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{in}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{in}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+t\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ex}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s\sim\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+t\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(s,f)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
F_{1}\sim F_{2}
denotes

C^{-1}e^{-CT}F_{1}\leq F_{2}\leq Ce^{CT}F_{1}

for some positive finite constant
C
, which depends only on
(\delta_{*},\delta^{*},\mathrm{E}_{1},\mathrm{E}_{2},\rho_{0},\gamma,\delta,\varepsilon_{0})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we only show that

(C.5)

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f)\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f)\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

and the rest of this lemma can be proved analogously. Moreover, note that

(C.6)

f_{r}=\eta_{r}D_{\eta}f,\qquad f_{tr}=\eta_{r}D_{\eta}f_{t},

we directly obtain

\big|\zeta r(f,\mathscr{D}_{r}f,f_{t},\mathscr{D}_{r}f_{t})\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(f,f_{t})\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(f_{r},f_{tr})\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f),

and thus we only need to focus on the estimates for the higher spatial derivatives of
f
.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Estimates for
\eta
.
First, in view of
\eqref{eq:VFBP-La-eta}_{2}
, we have

(C.7)

\displaystyle\eta_{trr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}U+\eta_{r}^{2}D_{\eta}^{2}U,\qquad\eta_{trrr}=\eta_{rrr}D_{\eta}U+3\eta_{r}\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}^{2}U+\eta_{r}^{3}D_{\eta}^{3}U,

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{tr}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\frac{U}{\eta}+\eta_{r}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),\qquad\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{tr}=\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}\frac{U}{\eta}+\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)^{2}\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big),

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{trr}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr}\frac{U}{\eta}+2\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{rr}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{r}^{2}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{U}{\eta}\big).

On the other hand, it follows from (
C.3
) and Lemmas
A.4
–
A.5
that

(C.8)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}U|_{\infty}\leq C\big|(\mathscr{D}_{\eta}U,\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}U)\big|_{1}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{3}\Big(\big|\chi r\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}\Big)\leq C,

(C.9)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}U|_{\infty}\leq C\sum_{j=2}^{4}\Big(\big|\chi r\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}D_{\eta}^{j}U\big|_{2}\Big)\leq C\mathcal{D}(t,U)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As a consequence, (
C.7
), together with (
C.3
), (
C.9
), and the Hölder inequality, implies that

(C.10)

|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}\eta|_{\infty}\leq Ce^{Ct}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}U|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\sqrt{t}e^{Ct},

and

|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{3}\eta|\leq Ce^{Ct}\Big(|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}\eta|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}U|\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{3}U|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq Ce^{Ct}\Big(t+\int_{0}^{t}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{3}U|\,\mathrm{d}s\Big),

which, along with the Minkowski inequality, leads to

(C.11)

|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{r}^{3}\eta|_{2}\leq Cte^{Ct}\big(1+\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq Cte^{Ct},\quad\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}\eta_{rrr}\big|_{2}\leq Cte^{Ct}\big(1+\mathrm{E}_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq Cte^{Ct}.

2. Estimates for
f
.
Now, a direct calculation gives

\displaystyle f_{rr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}f+\eta_{r}^{2}D_{\eta}^{2}f,\qquad f_{rrr}=\eta_{rrr}D_{\eta}f+3\eta_{r}\eta_{rr}D_{\eta}^{2}f+\eta_{r}^{3}D_{\eta}^{3}f,

\displaystyle\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}

\displaystyle=\eta_{r}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r},\qquad\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{r}=\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)^{2}\frac{1}{\eta}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\frac{1}{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r},

\displaystyle\big(\frac{f}{r}\big)_{rr}

\displaystyle=\eta_{rr}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+2\eta_{r}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{r}D_{\eta}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\eta_{r}^{2}\frac{\eta}{r}D_{\eta}^{2}\big(\frac{f}{\eta}\big)+\frac{f}{\eta}\big(\frac{\eta}{r}\big)_{rr},

which, combined with (
C.10
), yields

(C.12)

|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}f|\leq Ce^{Ct}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{2}f|,\qquad|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{3}f|\leq Ce^{Ct}|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}^{3}f|+|\mathscr{D}_{r}^{3}\eta||\mathscr{D}_{\eta}f|.

Finally, repeating the same calculation (
C.8
), we have
|\mathscr{D}_{\eta}f|_{\infty}\leq C(\mathcal{E}(t,f))^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Hence, this, together with (
C.11
)–(
C.12
), implies

|\zeta r\mathscr{D}_{r}^{2}f|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{in}}(t,f),\qquad\big|\chi^{\sharp}\rho_{0}^{(\gamma-1)(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})}(f_{rr},f_{rrr})\big|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{Ct}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ex}}(t,f).

This completes the proof of Lemma
C.1
.
∎

Appendix D
Cross-Derivatives Embedding

The following embedding theorem is used to obtain the higher-order elliptic estimates.

Lemma D.1
.

Assume that
\varphi=\varphi(r)
is a function defined on
I
and satisfies

(D.1)

\varphi\in C^{1}(\bar{I})\cap C^{2}((0,1]),\qquad\frac{1}{K}(1-r)\leq\varphi\leq K(1-r)\ \ \text{for some $K>1$}.

Let
(b,c)
be two parameters such that

(D.2)

\frac{1}{2}<b\leq\frac{c+1}{2},

and let
f=f(r)\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
satisfy both
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2q}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
for some
q\in[b,\frac{c+1}{2}]
and

(D.3)

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}<\infty.

Then, for any
\varphi
satisfying (
D.1
) and
(b,c)
satisfying (
D.2
), there exists a constant
C>0
, which depends only on
(\varphi,b,c)
, such that, for all
f
satisfying (
D.3
),

(D.4)

|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}\leq C\big(\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}\big).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Case
f\in C^{\infty}([\frac{1}{2},1])
.

It follows from integration by parts, (
D.1
)–(
D.3
), Lemma
A.6
, and the Young inequality that

(D.5)

\!\!\!\!\begin{aligned} |\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}^{2}&=\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}-c^{2}|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f|_{2}^{2}-2c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&=\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+c\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\quad+\underline{(2b-1-c)c\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}_{\,\leq 0}-\underline{c(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}f^{2}\Big|_{r=0}^{r=1}}_{\,=0}\\
&\leq\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+C\big(|(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi^{-1}\varphi_{r}|_{\infty}|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}^{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi_{rr}|_{\infty}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-\frac{1}{2}}f\big|_{2}^{2}\big)\\
&\leq\big|\zeta^{\sharp}(\varphi^{b}f_{r}+c\varphi^{b-1}\varphi_{r}f)\big|_{2}^{2}+C|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}f_{r}|_{2}^{2},\end{aligned}

which yields (
D.4
).

2. Case
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2b}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
In this case, we can repeat the calculation in (
D.5
) to derive (
D.4
), except for justifying the following integral equality:

(D.6)

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+(2b-1)\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Indeed, thanks to Lemma
A.1
, there exists a sequence
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\subset C^{\infty}([\frac{1}{2},1])
such that

(D.7)

|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)|_{2}+|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b}(f^{\varepsilon}_{r}-f_{r})|_{2}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$},

which, along with Lemma
A.5
, yields

(D.8)

|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-1}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)|_{2}+\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{b-\frac{1}{2}}(f^{\varepsilon}-f)\big|_{\infty}\to 0\qquad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Hence, according to (
D.7
)–(
D.8
) and integration by parts for
f^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(2\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}\varphi_{r}+(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi_{rr}\big)\varphi^{2b-1}(f^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+(2b-1)\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-2}(\varphi_{r})^{2}(f^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
implies that (
D.6
) holds for all
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{2b}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
.

This completes the proof of (
D.4
) when
q=b
.

3. General case.

It suffices to establish (
D.4
) when (
D.3
) holds and

b<\frac{c+1}{2},\qquad q=\frac{c+1}{2},\qquad\ f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}\big(\frac{1}{2},1\big),

due to the fact that
H^{1}_{\varphi^{2q}}(\frac{1}{2},1)\subset H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
if
q\leq\frac{c+1}{2}
. Note that, in this case, integration by parts in (
D.6
) fails owing to
(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}\varphi^{2b-1}\varphi_{r}ff_{r}\notin L^{1}
.

To overcome this difficulty, set

\vartheta:=\frac{c+1}{2}-b,\qquad\varphi_{j}:=\varphi+\frac{1}{j}\quad\text{for $j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}$}.

We first show a variant of (
A.4
) in Lemma
A.6
, that is, for all
f\in H^{1}_{\varphi^{c+1}}(\frac{1}{2},1)
,

(D.9)

\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\big(\big|\chi^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f\big|_{2}\big|\zeta^{\sharp}\varphi^{\frac{c+1}{2}}\varphi_{j}^{-\vartheta}f_{r}\big|_{2}\big).

Based on Lemma
A.1
and the proof in Lemma
A.6
, it suffices to show that (
D.9
) holds for
f\in C^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Clearly, we can further let
(\varphi,\varphi_{j})=(d,d_{j})
with
d=d(r):=1-r
and
d_{j}:=d+\frac{1}{j}
, due to

\frac{d}{K}\leq\varphi\leq Kd,\qquad\frac{d_{j}}{K}\leq\varphi_{j}\leq Kd_{j}.

It follows from the above reductions, integration by parts, and
\mathrm{d}(d^{c+1})_{r}=(c+1)d^{c}\mathrm{d}r
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{c+1}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\zeta^{\sharp}(\zeta^{\sharp})_{r}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta}ff_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\vartheta}{c+1}\int_{0}^{1}(\zeta^{\sharp})^{2}d^{c+1}d_{j}^{-2\vartheta-1}f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r
